| DEC-0783 | A stage result names the agent that produced it | - Date: 2026-09-26 - Adopted by the orchestrator on the implementation-reviewer's finding on the workflow control-core slice. - Why: BR-0553 and BR-0580 judge a reviewer by the run's history of authors and recommenders, but no payload named who authored a result, so the history stayed empty: `reviewer-not-independent` never fired and `finish` accepted an author's own `qa-gatekeeper` PASS. - Decision: every stage result, a routing result included, carries a required `actor: { agentInstance }`; the core records it as the recommender of a routing result and the author of any other, and each reviewer of an accepted result as a reviewer, and refuses a review by the result's own actor (BR-0747, EX-0001-0194-08). The least change that makes the two rules enforceable; the payload is new in this release, so no adopter payload breaks. - Rejected: inferring the author from the executor skill, which names a skill rather than an agent instance and cannot tell two instances of one skill apart. | DONE |
| DEC-0784 | A repair whose owner no active stage serves returns the run to routing | - Date: 2026-09-26 - Adopted by the orchestrator on the conflict between the journey tests and the implementation review of the workflow control core. - Why: a `test_fix` result that would change what its test checks hands the finding to `qfai-sdd` (BR-0581), but in a `bugfix` run the plan's one `qfai-sdd` stage is `sdd_append`, which the run skipped and whose operation only appends an example; it cannot change a criterion. - Decision: `next` routes a repair to the active stage its owner serves, and a finding whose owner no active stage serves takes the `running` to `routing` edge `scope-or-obligation-revision`, so routing settles a plan whose stage can make the change. The state machine row says `a repair whose owner no active stage of the plan serves`. - Rejected: issuing the skipped `sdd_append` stage to carry a change its operation does not allow. | DONE |
| DEC-0785 | The Windows parity job runs the workflow control-core suites | - Date: 2026-09-26 - Adopted by the orchestrator, settling OQ-0195. - Why: the control core writes the journal, the lock and path identities, which is where a Windows checkout differs, and `main` ran the same two directories in the job, which passes. - Decision: `test:windows-parity` runs `tests/unit/workflow/` and `tests/integration/workflow/` beside the init and migration suites (BR-0694, EX-0002-0024-01). OQ-0195 is DONE. - Rejected: naming single journal, lock and path suites, which a new suite in either directory would silently miss. | DONE |
| DEC-0786 | A spent replan budget blocks a run in `ready` | - Date: 2026-09-26 - Adopted by the orchestrator on the cross-slice review of the workflow control core. - Why: BR-0613 says reaching a replan budget leaves the run `blocked`, but a stale routing receipt found by `next` or `resume` on a run in `ready` had no edge to `blocked`, so the core refused the call and left the run `ready`. - Decision: conform to BR-0613. The state machine gains the `ready` to `blocked` edge `budget-exhausted` for that case; `next` and `resume` report the blocked run and its halt the way any blocked run is reported, and the error document keeps its shape (EX-0001-0196-25). - Rejected: a refusal carrying a halt, which contradicted BR-0613 and the Output section. | DONE |
| DEC-0787 | A busy or refused run file is `io-error` whether read or written | - Date: 2026-09-26 - Adopted by the orchestrator on the completion-reviewer's finding on the workflow follow-ups. - Why: the core answers `io-error` for `EBUSY`, `EPERM` and `EACCES` raised while it reads a run file as well as while it writes one, while the contract named writes only. The operator can act on either the same way: close whatever holds the file or restore its permissions, and run the operation again. - Decision: the `io-error` row and the journal bullet of `qfai-workflow.md` say "while reading or writing a run file", and EX-0001-0196-10 covers a read. It widens DEC-0768 to reads. - Rejected: letting a refused read escape as an exception with no document. | DONE |
| DEC-0788 | Change request: .qfai/spec/03_contract/cli/qfai-prototyping.md, .qfai/spec/03_contract/cli/qfai-prototyping-iterate.md, .qfai/spec/02_business-flow/business-flow-0001/user-story-0001-0117/03_Example.md | - Date: 2026-09-26 - Approved by the user on 2026-09-26 through the structured question tool. - Why: BR-0267 says `design-system.yaml` is generated post-loop as a deterministic mirror of the root `DESIGN.md` token tables, but no command wrote it; the `/qfai-prototyping` skill had the orchestrating agent write it by hand, which is neither deterministic nor checked before `qfai validate` reads it. - Options: 1. `qfai prototyping iterate` writes the file on the cycle that ends the loop, the convergence stop (exit 64) or the budget stop (exit 65), from the root `DESIGN.md` that invocation read (recommended, adopted). 2. A new public sub-command, `qfai prototyping mirror`, run by the skill after the loop (not selected: a fourth public sub-command for a step that has exactly one moment to run). 3. Keep the hand-written step (not selected: the defect this row answers). - Decision: option 1. An unreadable or malformed `DESIGN.md`, and a lock that is malformed, unreadable or differs from `DESIGN.md`, already exit 2 before the stop is evaluated, so nothing is written. A missing lock is allowed by iterate as before; the mirror records the hash of the `DESIGN.md` it copies, which equals the lock's whenever a lock exists. The skill's hand-written step is removed. - Changes: the iterate section and BR-0267 of `qfai-prototyping.md`, the convergence section of `qfai-prototyping-iterate.md`, and EX-0001-0117-01, whose "Post-handoff" timing becomes the cycle that ends the loop. | DONE |
| DEC-0789 | `/qfai-sdd` checks its rules against its examples in a cycle between the contracts and the gate | - Date: 2026-09-26 - Decided by the user on 2026-09-26 in a confirmed discussion session. - Decision: after Stage 4 writes the contracts and their BRs, and before the per-flow gate, `/qfai-sdd` runs a concrete-abstract cycle. One cycle has four steps: the BRs are written from the EXs; an agent reads the BRs and raises findings against the concrete items; each finding is adopted or rejected; the adopted changes are applied to BF, US, AC and EX, and the BRs are rewritten from the updated EXs (BR-0748, BR-0750). | DONE |
| DEC-0790 | The cycle's finder is a test-design-analyst that wrote none of the BRs, and it raises five kinds of finding | - Date: 2026-09-26 - Decided by the user on 2026-09-26 in a confirmed discussion session. - Decision: the finder is an agent other than the BR author, a test-design-analyst. It raises five kinds of finding: a case a BR's conditions imply that no EX states, such as a boundary, the negative side or a combination of conditions; a redundant EX that exercises the same BR partition as another; an EX no BR explains; a BR its cited EXs do not support, over- or under-generalized; and a BF, US or AC granularity or split the BRs show to be wrong (BR-0748, BR-0754). | DONE |
| DEC-0791 | A finding the written tree settles is decided by an independent agent, and a finding resting on product intent by the user | - Date: 2026-09-26 - Decided by the user on 2026-09-26 in a confirmed discussion session. - Decision: each finding is adopted or rejected. A finding derivable from the existing BRs, ACs or the request is decided by an independent agent taking the griller's recommendation. A finding resting on product intent that nothing written states goes to the user, as a critical decision under `.agents/rules/grilling.md#critical-decisions` (BR-0749). | DONE |
| DEC-0792 | An adopted finding changes the story tree only by the routes that already govern each item | - Date: 2026-09-26 - Decided by the user on 2026-09-26 in a confirmed discussion session. - Decision: an item written in this run may be changed directly. Under `--contract`, and in a workflow run, a finding that would change a story becomes a change request under the existing rules; in a run that is the per-change approval of DEC-0748 and DEC-0778 (BR-0750, BR-0751). | DONE |

The completion reviewer checks that record, and no new validator is added (BR-0754). | DONE |
| DEC-0796 | The cycle adds no case the request did not need | - Date: 2026-09-26 - Decided by the user on 2026-09-26 in a confirmed discussion session. - Decision: a proposed EX must be implied by an existing BR, an existing AC or the request, and one that is not is rejected, as `.agents/rules/grilling.md#the-request-bounds-the-tree` requires (BR-0749). | DONE |
| DEC-0797 | The cycle's remaining rules were settled by a delegated grilling session | - Date: 2026-09-26 - Adopted by the orchestrator from the recommendation of the requirements-reviewer acting as griller, after two rounds with the requirements-analyst and the solution-architect. - Why: the user's decisions DEC-0789 to DEC-0796 left these points open, and the cycle cannot be specified without them. - Decision: (1) the finder reads the BRs whose Statement or Examples cell the invocation wrote or changed, the EXs they cite and the EXs the invocation wrote or changed, and no cycle runs when no such BR exists (BR-0748). (2) No cycle runs under `sdd_append`, because BR-0734 forbids the changes most findings would make (BR-0748). (3) A finding from either cycle that has no decision when the loop ends becomes an `Unadjudicated:` row of `open-questions.md`, opened at once under `--auto` (BR-0752). (4) In a workflow run the cycle runs on the proposal before the one change question of BR-0741, and an adopted finding outside the run's checked scope makes the stage return `blocked` (BR-0751). (5) The finder raises no finding whose kind and target IDs match a finding already decided in the invocation, a REJECTED row or a `Change request:` row at TODO (BR-0753). (6) One griller per cycle adjudicates, and it is neither the finder nor an author of a targeted item (BR-0749). (7) The Work Orders Summary names the finder, and the completion reviewer returns REVISE on an unapproved change to an item that existed when the invocation started (BR-0754). - Dissent on (6): the requirements-analyst would have the finder grill with a recommendation, and a separate agent only classify each finding as critical or not and apply the scope bound. | DONE |
